(2.7)

such that

(f_{!}\dashv f^{\ast})
is a
\mathrm{sSet}_{\ast}
-Quillen adjunction for the stable model structures.
In addition:

•

If
f
is a weak equivalence then
(f_{!}\dashv f^{\ast})
is a
\mathrm{sSet}_{\ast}
-Quillen equivalence.

•

If
f
is a fibration then
(f^{\ast}\dashv f_{\ast})
is a
\mathrm{sSet}_{\ast}
-Quillen adjunction for the stable model structures, which is moreover a Quillen equivalence if
f
is acyclic.

Remark 2.24
.

A
\mathrm{sSet}
-Quillen adjunction of simplicial model categories
(L\dashv R)\colon\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{N}
induces an adjunction of
\infty
-categories
(L\dashv R)\colon\mathcal{M}^{\infty}\to\mathcal{N}^{\infty}
.
In this manner the
\mathrm{sSet}_{\ast}
-Quillen adjunctions of (
2.7
) are identified with the adjunctions (
2.1
).

For a reduced simplicial set
X
we use Kan’s simplicial monoid
\mathbb{G}X
to model the homotopy type of
\Omega X
.
By means of the
\mathrm{sSet}_{\ast}
-tensoring on sequential spectra we form a model category of sequential
\mathbb{G}X_{+}
-module spectra; that is, of sequential spectra with a
\mathbb{G}X_{+}
-action.
The resulting model category, denoted
\mathbb{G}X_{+}\mathrm{-Mod}^{\mathbb{N}}
, is isomorphic to the model category of sequential spectra in pointed
\mathbb{G}X
-spaces.

Proposition 2.25
.

For any reduced simplicial set
X
, there is a
\mathrm{sSet}_{\ast}
-Quillen equivalence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}X_{+}\mathrm{-Mod}^{\mathbb{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}^{\mathbb{N}}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{b}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{s}_{X}}

Sketch of proof.

The adjunction in question is found in
[
Bra20a
, Remark 2.13]
.
The proof that this adjunction is a Quillen equivalence is sketched in
loc. cit.
, alternatively apply the sequential stabilisation machine to
[
Bra20a
, Lemma 1.31]
.
∎

Remark 2.26
.

The functor
\mathfrak{b}_{X}
sends a
\mathbb{G}X_{+}
-module spectrum
E
to the
X
-spectrum
x_{!}(E/\mathbb{G}X)
, with
x\colon\ast\to X
the unique
0
-simplex.
The Quillen equivalence of Proposition
2.25
thus identifies the pushforward functor
x_{!}\colon\mathrm{Sp}^{\mathbb{N}}\to\mathrm{Sp}^{\mathbb{N}}_{X}
with the free functor
P\mapsto\mathbb{G}X_{+}\wedge P
.
In addition, for any fibrant
\Omega_{X}
-spectrum
A
,
\mathfrak{s}_{X}A
has underlying spectrum equivalent to
x^{\ast}A
, the fibre spectrum of
A
at
x\in X
.

The next result uses the combinatorial models to characterise fibrewise Eilenberg–Mac Lane spectra.

Proposition 2.27
.

Let
X
be a connected space with
x\in X
.
For any
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, the functor

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sp}_{X}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]\mathrm{-Mod}

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\longmapsto\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}(x^{\ast}P)

restricts to an equivalence on the full subcategory of
X
-spectra with fibrewise stable homotopy concentrated in dimension
k
.

Proof.

By applying fibrewise suspension or looping functors as necessary, we may suppose that
k=0
without loss of generality.
We may also take
X
to be a reduced simplicial set with unique
0
-simplex
x
, in which case Proposition
2.25
shows that the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Sp}_{X}
is presented by the model category
\mathbb{G}X_{+}\mathrm{-Mod}^{\mathbb{N}}
of sequential
\mathbb{G}X_{+}
-module spectra.
By Remark
2.26
, the functor
P\mapsto\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{n}(x^{\ast}P)
on
X
-spectra is modelled in terms of
\mathbb{G}X_{+}
-module spectra simply by computing
n
-th stable homotopy groups.

Let
E
be a sequential
\mathbb{G}X_{+}
-module spectrum corresponding to the
X
-spectrum
P
, taking
E
to be cofibrant-fibrant without loss of generality.
The structure map

\rho_{E}\colon\mathbb{G}X_{+}\wedge E\longrightarrow E

furnishes
\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{st}}E
with the structure of an
\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]
-module via the action map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{0}\mathbb{G}X]\otimes\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{0}E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{st}}\mathbb{G}X\otimes\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{0}E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbb{G}X_{+}\wedge E)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{st}}E\,,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{0}\rho_{E}}

using
\pi_{0}\mathbb{G}X\cong\pi_{1}X
.
Upon taking zeroth stable homotopy groups, morphisms of
\mathbb{G}X_{+}
-module spectra give rise to morphisms of
\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]
-modules and thus the functor
\mathrm{Sp}_{X}\to\mathbb{Z}\mathrm{-Mod}
that sends
P\mapsto\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{0}(x^{\ast}P)
factors via the functor
\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]\mathrm{-Mod}\to\mathbb{Z}\mathrm{-Mod}
induced by the canonical augmentation
\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]\to\mathbb{Z}
.

Suppose that the
X
-spectra
A
and
B
are (
0
-)connective and (
0
-)coconnective respectively.
By the above discussion there is a homomorphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{A,B\}^{0}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]}\big(\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{0}(x^{\ast}A),\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{0}(x^{\ast}B)\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varpi_{A,B}}

which we claim is an isomorphism.
To see this, consider the full subcategory
\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow Ho(\mathrm{Sp}_{X})
of connective
X
-spectra
A
for which
\varpi_{A,B}
is an isomorphism for all coconnective
X
-spectra
B
.
The subcategory
\mathcal{E}
contains
x_{!}S
, for
\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{0}(x^{\ast}x_{!}S)=\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]
and thus for any coconnective
X
-spectrum
B
,
\varpi_{x_{!}S,B}
is the evident isomorphism

\{x_{!}S,B\}^{0}_{X}\cong\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{0}(x^{\ast}B)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]}\big(\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X],\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{0}(x^{\ast}B)\big)\,.

The category
\mathcal{E}
is readily seen to be closed under forming sums and cofibre sequences, hence under all colimits.
By Proposition
2.11
we have that
\mathcal{E}
is the full subcategory spanned by connective
X
-spectra.

An easy consequence of the above is that the assignment
P\mapsto\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{0}(x^{\ast}P)
becomes fully faithful when restricted to the homotopy category of
X
-spectra with fibrewise stable homotopy concentrated in dimension zero.
To show that this functor is also essentially surjective, we once more use objects of
\mathbb{G}X_{+}\mathrm{-Mod}^{\mathbb{N}}
to model
X
-spectra.
For a
\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]
-module
M
, first form the sequential Eilenberg–Mac Lane spectrum
HM
whose
n
-th space
HM_{n}=M[S^{n}]
is the reduced
M
-linearisation of the simplicial
n
-sphere
S^{n}
, equipped with sequential spectrum structure maps

\displaystyle S^{1}\wedge M[S^{n}]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow M[S^{n+1}]

\displaystyle x\wedge\Big(\sum_{i}m_{i}y_{i}\Big)

\displaystyle\longmapsto\sum_{i}m_{i}(x\wedge y_{i})\,.

We use the
\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]
-module structure of
M
to equip each space
M[S^{n}]
with
\mathbb{G}X_{+}
-structure maps

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}X_{+}\wedge M[S^{n}]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow M[S^{n}]

\displaystyle\gamma\wedge\Big(\sum_{i}m_{i}y_{i}\Big)

\displaystyle\longmapsto\sum_{i}([\gamma]\cdot m_{i})y_{i}\,,

where
[\gamma]
is the class of
\gamma
in
\pi_{0}\mathbb{G}X\cong\pi_{1}X
.
These
\mathbb{G}X_{+}
-structure maps are clearly compatible with the suspension spectrum structure maps, so that
HM
is a (fibrant) sequential
\mathbb{G}X_{+}
-module spectrum.
By fibrancy, in order to compute zeroth stable homotopy groups of
HM
it is sufficient to take unstable
\pi_{0}
in dimension zero, where we recover
M
with its given structure as a
\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]
-module.
∎

Remark 2.28
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a map of connected spaces.
Under the equivalence of Proposition
2.27
, the restriction of
f^{\ast}\colon\mathrm{Sp}_{Y}\to\mathrm{Sp}_{X}
to full subcategories of parametrised spectra with fibrewise stable homotopy concentrated in dimension zero is identified with the base change functor

\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}Y]\mathrm{-Mod}\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]\mathrm{-Mod}

induced by
\pi_{1}f\colon\pi_{1}X\to\pi_{1}Y
.
In the case that
Y=\ast
, this implies that any
X
-parametrised spectrum
P
with fibrewise stable homotopy concentrated in degree zero for which
\pi_{1}X
acts trivially on
\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{0}x^{\ast}P
must be stably equivalent to
X^{\ast}HA
for some abelian group
A
.

2.2
Nilpotent parametrised spectra

For connected
X
, we will call an
X
-parametrised spectrum
P

nilpotent
if
\pi_{1}X
acts nilpotently on each fibrewise stable homotopy group
\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}(x^{\ast}P)
.
This condition turns out to be equivalent to requiring that each stage Postnikov tower of
P
factors as a finite sequence of extensions by untwisted Eilenberg–Mac Lane spectra.

Recall that an action
\alpha\colon\pi\to\mathrm{Aut}(G)
of
\pi
on a group
G
is
nilpotent
if there is a finite sequence of subgroups of
G

G=G_{0}\geq G_{1}\geq\dotsb\geq G_{j}\geq\dotsb\geq G_{q}=\ast

such that for each
j
:

(i)

G_{j}
is closed under the action
\alpha
,

(ii)

G_{j+1}\trianglelefteq G_{j}
is normal with abelian quotient, and

(iii)

The induced action of
\pi
on
G_{j}/G_{j+1}
is trivial.

We are frequently concerned with the case that
G=A
is an abelian group, i.e. a
\mathbb{Z}[\pi]
-module.
We shall need the following easy lemma:

Lemma 2.29
.

If
\pi
acts on a short exact sequence of groups

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ast}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ast}

then the action of
\pi
on
G
is nilpotent if and only if the actions of
\pi
on
G^{\prime}
and
G^{\prime\prime}
are nilpotent.

Remark 2.30
.

A fibration with
p\colon E\to B
with connected fibre
F
is
nilpotent
if the action of
\pi_{1}E
on each
\pi_{k}F
is nilpotent.
A fibration
p\colon E\to B
is nilpotent if and only if every stage of the Moore–Postnikov tower of
p
can be factored as the composite of finitely many principal fibrations with fibres
K(\pi,n)
’s (see
[
BK72
, Section III.5]
).

A connected space
X
is nilpotent if the terminal map
X\to\ast
is nilpotent; equivalently, if
\pi_{1}X
is a nilpotent group and the
\pi_{1}X
-action on
\pi_{n}X
is nilpotent for all
n\geq 2
.

Definition 2.31
.

Let
X
be a connected space with
x\in X
.
An
X
-parametrised spectrum
P
with fibre spectrum
x^{\ast}P
at
x
is called
nilpotent
if the action of
\pi_{1}X
on each
\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}(x^{\ast}P)
is nilpotent.
We write
Ho(\mathrm{Sp}_{X})_{\mathrm{nil}}\hookrightarrow Ho(\mathrm{Sp}_{X})
for the full subcategory on nilpotent parametrised spectra.

Example 2.32
.

Any
X
-spectrum in the image of the pullback functor
X^{\ast}\colon Ho(\mathrm{Sp})\to Ho(\mathrm{Sp}_{X})
is nilpotent since
\Omega X_{+}
acts trivially on the fibre spectrum (cf. Remark
2.28
).

Proposition 2.33
.

For a connected space
X
and fibre sequence of
X
-parametrised spectra
A\to B\to C
,
if any two of
A
,
B
, or
C
is nilpotent then so is the third.

Proof.

Taking fibre spectra yields a fibre sequence
x^{\ast}A\to x^{\ast}B\to x^{\ast}C
, which in turn induces a long exact sequence of stable homotopy groups

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k+1}x^{\ast}C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}x^{\ast}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}x^{\ast}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}x^{\ast}C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k-1}x^{\ast}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}

in which each arrow is a homomorphism of
\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]
-modules.
Using Lemma
2.29
, it follows that if any two of
\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{\ast}x^{\ast}A
,
\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{\ast}x^{\ast}B
, and
\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{\ast}x^{\ast}C
are nilpotent
\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]
-modules then so is the third.
∎

Corollary 2.34
.

For a connected space
X
,

Ho(\mathrm{Sp}_{X})_{\mathrm{nil}}\hookrightarrow Ho(\mathrm{Sp}_{X})

is a triangulated subcategory.

We shall require the following useful characterisation of nilpotent
X
-spectra:

Theorem 2.35
.

An
X
-spectrum
P
is nilpotent if and only if for all
k
, the zero morphism
P_{=k}\to 0_{X}
factors as a finite sequence of extensions by
X^{\ast}(\Sigma^{k}HA)
’s.

Proof.

For the “if” direction, suppose that
P
is an
X
-spectrum such that for each integer
k
, the map
P_{=k}\to 0_{X}
factors as a finite sequence of extensions by
X^{\ast}(\Sigma^{k}HA)
’s.
Note that fibre spectrum
x^{\ast}{P_{=k}}
of
P_{=k}
is equivalent to
(x^{\ast}{P})_{=k}
(Corollary
2.17
) and by hypothesis there is a diagram of
X
-spectra

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{=k}=(P_{=k})_{n_{k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(P_{=k})_{n_{k}-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(P_{=k})_{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(P_{=k})_{j-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(P_{=k})_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\ast}\Sigma^{k}HA_{n_{k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\ast}\Sigma^{k}HA_{n_{k}-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\ast}\Sigma^{k}HA_{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\ast}\Sigma^{k}HA_{j-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\ast}\Sigma^{k}HA_{1}}

in which each
X^{\ast}\Sigma^{k}HA_{j}\to(P_{=k})_{j}\to(P_{=k})_{j-1}
is a fibre sequence.
As each
X^{\ast}\Sigma^{k}HA_{j}
is nilpotent, Proposition
2.33
implies that
P_{=k}
is also.
Each of the zig-zags
P\leftarrow P_{\geq k}\to P_{=k}
induces an isomorphism of
\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]
-modules
\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}x^{\ast}P\cong\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}x^{\ast}P_{=k}
and hence
P
is nilpotent.

Conversely, suppose
P
is a nilpotent
X
-spectrum.
Fixing an integer
k
, there is a filtration of
\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}x^{\ast}{P}
by subgroups

\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}x^{\ast}{P}=\Gamma_{0}\geq\Gamma_{1}\geq\dotsb\geq\Gamma_{j}\geq\dotsb\geq\Gamma_{n_{k}}=0

such that each
\Gamma_{j}\to\Gamma_{j-1}
is an injective homomorphism of
\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]
-modules for which the
\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]
-action on
\Gamma_{j-1}/\Gamma_{j}
is trivial (that is, the action factors through the augmentation
\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]\to\mathbb{Z}
).
Recall that the adjoint equivalence of
\infty
-categories
(\mathfrak{b}_{X}\dashv\mathfrak{s}_{X})\colon\Omega X_{+}\mathrm{-Mod}\to\mathrm{Sp}_{X}
of Propositions
2.2
and
2.25
identifies
P
with the stable fibre
x^{\ast}P
equipped with the Serre action of
\Omega X_{+}
.
Since the fibre spectrum
x^{\ast}{P_{=k}}
of
P_{=k}
is equivalent to
(x^{\ast}P)_{=k}
, Proposition
2.27
implies that for each
0\leq j\leq n_{k}
there is a morphism of
\Omega X_{+}
-module spectra
\Sigma^{k}H\Gamma_{j}\to\mathfrak{s}_{X}P_{=k}
which corresponds to the morphism
\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]
-modules
\Gamma_{j}\to\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}x^{\ast}P
by taking stable homotopy groups.
Extending to a cofibre sequence
\Sigma^{k}H\Gamma_{j}\to\mathfrak{s}_{X}P_{=k}\to Q_{j}
, we obtain a sequence of
\Omega X_{+}
-module spectra

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{s}_{X}P_{=k}\cong Q_{n_{k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q_{n_{k}-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q_{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q_{0}\cong 0}

where
Q_{j}\to Q_{j-1}
induces the morphism of
\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]
-modules
(\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}x^{\ast}P)/\Gamma_{j}\to(\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}x^{\ast}P)/\Gamma_{j-1}
on homotopy.
Taking fibres of the morphisms
Q_{j}\to Q_{j-1}
, we get fibre sequences of
\Omega X_{+}
-module spectra
\Sigma^{k}H(\Gamma_{j-1}/\Gamma_{j})\to Q_{j}\to Q_{j-1}
for which the
\Sigma^{k}H(\Gamma_{j-1}/\Gamma_{j})
are trivial
\Omega X_{+}
-module spectra.
By Remark
2.28
the adjoint equivalence of
\infty
-categories
(\mathfrak{b}_{X}\dashv\mathfrak{s}_{X})\colon\Omega X_{+}\mathrm{-Mod}\to\mathrm{Sp}_{X}
identifies the trivial
\Omega X_{+}
-module spectra
\Sigma^{k}H(\Gamma_{j-1}/\Gamma_{j})
with
X^{\ast}\Sigma^{k}H(\Gamma_{j-1}/\Gamma_{j})

so that we get a diagram of
X
-spectra

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{=k}\cong\mathfrak{b}_{X}Q_{n_{k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{b}_{X}Q_{n_{k}-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{b}_{X}Q_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\ast}\Sigma^{k}H\Gamma_{n_{k}-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\ast}\Sigma^{k}H(\Gamma_{n_{k}-2}/\Gamma_{n_{k}-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\ast}\Sigma^{k}H(\Gamma_{0}/\Gamma_{1})}

in which each
X^{\ast}\Sigma^{k}H(\Gamma_{j-1}/\Gamma_{j})\to\mathfrak{b}_{X}Q_{j}\to\mathfrak{b}_{X}Q_{j-1}
is a fibre sequence.
∎

Corollary 2.36
.

The class of nilpotent
X
-spectra is closed under finite sums.

Proof.

Suppose that
P^{1},\dotsc,P^{n}
are nilpotent
X
-spectra.
For each integer
k
we have
(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}P^{i})_{=k}\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}P^{i}_{=k}
.
By Theorem
2.35
, for each
1\leq i\leq n
there is an integer
m_{i}
and sequence of abelian groups
HA^{i}_{1},\dotsc,HA^{i}_{m_{i}}
such that for each
1\leq j\leq m_{i}
there are fibre sequences of
X
-spectra

X^{\ast}\Sigma^{k}HA^{i}_{j}\longrightarrow(P^{i}_{=k})_{j}\longrightarrow(P^{i}_{=k})_{j-1}

with
(P^{i}_{=k})_{m_{i}}=P^{i}_{=k}
and
(P^{i}_{=k})_{0}=0
.
Letting
m:=\max\{m_{1},\dotsb,m_{n}\}
, for each
i
and
m_{i}<j\leq m
we set
HA^{i}_{j}:=0
and
(P^{i}_{=k})_{j}:=P^{i}_{=k}
.
Then for each
1\leq j\leq m
we have a fibre sequence of
X
-spectra

\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}X^{\ast}\Sigma^{k}HA^{i}_{j}\cong X^{\ast}\Sigma^{k}H\bigg(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}A^{i}_{j}\bigg)\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(P^{i}_{=k})_{j}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(P^{i}_{=k})_{j-1}

with
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(P^{i}_{=k})_{0}=0
and
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(P^{i}_{=k})_{m}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}P^{i}_{=k}
.
Theorem
2.35
now shows that
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}P^{i}
is nilpotent.
∎

Corollary 2.37
.

An
X
-spectrum
P
is nilpotent if and only if
P_{\leq k}
is nilpotent for all
k
.

Proof.

For any
X
-spectrum
P
and integer
k
, by (
2.2
) the
k
-th slice
P_{=k}
is the fibre of the map of Postnikov sections
P_{\leq k}\to P_{\leq(k-1)}
.
For any
l\leq k
the
l
-th Postnikov sections of
P
and
P_{\leq k}
coincide:
(P_{\leq k})_{\leq l}\cong P_{\leq l}
.
Hence
P_{=l}\cong(P_{\leq k})_{=l}
for all
l\leq k
and the result follows by Theorem
2.35
.
∎

We conclude this section with a result that provides a natural class of examples of nilpotent parametrised spectra.

Proposition 2.38
.

For a connected space
X
, the fibrewise stabilisation of a nilpotent fibration
E\to X
is a nilpotent parametrised spectrum.

Proof.

Let
p\colon E\to B
be a nilpotent fibration with fibre
F
, and let
E\to E_{\leq k}\to X
be the
k
-th stage of the Moore–Postnikov tower of
p
.
Define
E^{(k)}
as the pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{\leq k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{(k)}\,,}

so that
p
factors as
E\to E_{\leq k}\to E^{(k)}\to X
.
Write
F_{(\leq k)}
and
F^{(k)}
for the fibres of
E_{\leq k}\to X
and
E^{(k)}\to X
respectively, and observe that we have a cofibre sequence of
X
-parametrised spectra
\Sigma^{\infty}_{X+}E\to\Sigma^{\infty}_{X+}E_{\leq k}\to\Sigma^{\infty}_{X+}E^{(k)}
.

For a fibre sequence
Z\to Y\to X
, the fibrewise stabilisation
\Sigma^{\infty}_{X+}Y
has fibre spectrum
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}Z
with
\pi_{1}X
acting via the Serre action on
\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}Z
(cf. Example
2.8
).
Upon passing to fibres, the cofibre (hence fibre) sequence of
X
-parametrised spectra
\Sigma^{\infty}_{X+}E\to\Sigma^{\infty}_{X+}E_{\leq k}\to\Sigma^{\infty}_{X+}E^{(k)}
induces a fibre sequence of spectra
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}F\to\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}F_{(\leq k)}\to\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}F^{(k)}
and hence a long exact sequence of stable homotopy groups

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{i+1}F^{(k)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{i}F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{i}F_{(\leq k)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{i}F^{(k)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{i-1}F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}

where each arrow is a homomorphism of
\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}X]
-modules.
But
X\to E^{(k)}
induces an isomorphism on
\pi_{i}
for
i<k
, from which it follows that the fibre
F^{(k)}
is
(k-2)
-connected and hence
\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{i}F^{(k)}=0
for
i\leq k-2
.
From the long exact sequence, it follows that
\Sigma^{\infty}_{X+}E\to\Sigma^{\infty}_{X+}E_{\leq k}
induces an isomorphism of fibrewise stable homotopy groups in dimensions
i<k-2
.

In order to show that
\Sigma^{\infty}_{X+}E
is a nilpotent
X
-parametrised spectrum, it is now sufficient to show that
\Sigma^{\infty}_{X+}E_{\leq k}
is so for each stage of the Moore–Postnikov tower
E\to E_{\leq k}\to X
.
Since
p\colon E\to X
is nilpotent,
E_{k}\to X
factors as a composite of finitely many principal fibrations with
K(\pi,n)
’s as fibres.
Lemmas
2.41
and
2.42
below show that
\Sigma^{\infty}_{X+}E_{\leq k}
is a nilpotent
X
-spectrum for all
k
.
∎

Essentially the same argument also proves

Proposition 2.39
.

Let
Z
be a retractive space over connected
X
for which the projection map
Z\to X
is a nilpotent fibration.
Then the fibrewise stabilisation
\Sigma^{\infty}_{X}Z
is a nilpotent parametrised spectrum.

Corollary 2.40
.

For a nilpotent space
X
with
x\in X
,
x_{!}S
is a compact generator of
Ho(\mathrm{Sp}_{X})_{\mathrm{nil}}
.

Proof.

For connected
X
, the fibrewise stabilisation of
x\colon\ast\to X
is a weak compact generator of
\mathrm{Sp}_{X}
(Corollary
2.3
), so is a weak generator of
Ho(\mathrm{Sp}_{X})_{\mathrm{nil}}
provided it is nilpotent.
For nilpotent
X
, the path fibration
PX\to X
is nilpotent and hence so is the fibrewise stabilisation
\Sigma^{\infty}_{X+}PX\cong x_{!}S
by the Proposition (cf. Example
2.7
(2)).
∎

We say a fibration
p\colon E\to B
with connected fibre
F
is
S
-good
if the Serre action of
\pi_{1}B
on
\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}F
is nilpotent for all
k\geq 0
.

Lemma 2.41
.

Let
f\colon Z\to Y
and
g\colon Y\to X
be nilpotent fibrations.
If
f
and
g
are
S
-good, then so is
g\circ f\colon Z\to X
.

Proof.

Letting
F(f)
,
F(g)
, and
F(gf)
be the fibres of
f
,
g
, and
g\circ f
respectively, there is a fibre sequence

F(f)\longrightarrow F(gf)\longrightarrow F(g)\,.

There is a convergent generalised homology spectral sequence

H_{p}(F(g);\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{q}F(f))\Rightarrow\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{p+q}F(gf)
upon which
\pi_{1}Z
acts.
To show that
\pi_{1}Z
(hence
\pi_{1}X
) acts nilpotently on each of the
\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}F(gf)
, by Lemma
2.29
it is sufficient to show that
\pi_{1}Z
acts nilpotently on each of the homology groups
H_{p}(F(g);\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{q}F(f))
.

Since
\pi_{1}Z
acts nilpotently on
\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{q}F(f)
, there is a filtration by subgroups

\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{q}F(f)=\Gamma_{0}\geq\Gamma_{1}\geq\dots\geq\Gamma_{j}\geq\dotsb\geq\Gamma_{n}=0

such that each
\Gamma_{j+1}\to\Gamma_{j}
is an injective homomorphism of
\pi_{1}Z
-modules and the quotient
\Gamma_{j}/\Gamma_{j+1}
has trivial
\pi_{1}Z
-action.
On the other hand, since
g
is a nilpotent fibration
\pi_{1}X
(and hence
\pi_{1}Z
) acts nilpotently on each
H_{p}F(g)

[
BK72
, Lemma II.5.4]
and so it follows that
\pi_{1}Z
acts nilpotently on
H_{p}F(g)\otimes(\Gamma_{j}/\Gamma_{j+1})
and
\mathrm{Tor}(H_{p-1}F(g),\Gamma_{j}/\Gamma_{j+1})
for each
p
.
The universal coefficient theorem and Lemma
2.29
together imply that
\pi_{1}Z
acts nilpotently on each
H_{p}(F(g);\Gamma_{j}/\Gamma_{j+1})
.
Working over coefficient sequences, it now follows that
\pi_{1}Z
acts nilpotently on each
H_{p}(F(g);\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{q}F(f))
.
∎

Lemma 2.42
.

Let
p\colon E\to B
be a principal fibration with fibre
K(A,n)
.
Then the Serre action of
\pi_{1}B
on
\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}K(A,n)
is trivial.

Proof.

A principal fibration of the form
K(A,n)\to E\to B
is simple.
Every element
\gamma
of
\pi_{1}B
induces a self-homotopy equivalence
\rho(\gamma)\colon K(A,n)\to K(A,n)
such that the action of
\pi_{1}B
on
\pi_{n}K(A,n)
is identified with the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[K(A,n),K(A,n)]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}(A,A)\,,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{n}}

where the latter map is an isomorphism.
Since the fibration is simple, each
\gamma\in\pi_{1}B
acts trivially on
\pi_{n}K(A,n)=A
and hence each self-homotopy equivalence
\rho(\gamma)\colon K(A,n)\to K(A,n)
must be homotopic to the identity.
The action of
\pi_{1}B
on
\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}K(A,n)
is identified with the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[K(A,n),K(A,n)]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}(\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}K(A,n),\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}K(A,n))\,,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}}

which must therefore be trivial.
∎

3
Sullivan’s rational homotopy theory

We now turn to the algebraic categories in which our models for rational homotopy types shall live.
Following
[
BG76
]
, we begin with a careful recollection of the model category of commutative differential graded algebras, the home of Sullivan’s rational models for topological spaces.

Definition 3.1
.

A
commutative differential graded algebra
(
cdga
) over
\mathbb{Q}
is a non-negatively graded rational cochain complex
(A^{\ast},d)
equipped with an associative unital graded commutative product
\mu\colon A\otimes A\to A
for which
d
is a graded derivation (of cohomological degree
1
).
The data
(A^{\ast},d,\mu)
are frequently abbreviated to
A
, with
d_{A}:=d
and
\mu_{A}:=\mu
the usual notation for the differential and multiplication of
A
.
We also frequently use the shorthand
a\cdot b:=\mu_{A}(a,b)
for the multiplication in
A
.

A morphism of cdgas
f\colon A\to B
is a map of cochain complexes
f\colon(A^{\ast},d_{A})\to(B^{\ast},d_{B})
that is compatible with multiplication maps in the sense that
\mu_{B}\circ(f\otimes f)=f\circ\mu_{A}
.

The collection of cdgas (over
\mathbb{Q}
) organises into a category, which we denote by
\mathrm{cDGA}
.

Example 3.2
.

There is a class of morphisms in
\mathrm{cDGA}
that plays a particularly important role in rational homotopy theory.
A morphism
f\colon A\to B
is
semifree
if the following conditions are satisfied:

(i)

The underlying graded commutative algebra of
B
is
B^{\ast}=A\otimes\Lambda V
, with
\Lambda V
the free graded-commutative algebra generated by a non-negatively graded rational vector space
V
.

(ii)

The graded rational vector space
V
has a basis
\{v_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\in\mathcal{I}\}
where
\mathcal{I}
is a well-ordered set and the differential
d_{B}
on
B^{\ast}=A\otimes\Lambda V
is such that for all
\beta\in\mathcal{I}
, the differential
d_{B}v_{\beta}
lies in the subcomplex
A\otimes\Lambda V_{<\beta}
with
V_{<\beta}=\mathrm{span}\{v_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\beta\}
.

(iii)

The morphism
f\colon A\to B
is given on underlying cochain complexes by the evident inclusion
A\to A\otimes\Lambda V
.

A semifree morphism
f\colon A\to B
is
minimal
if the basis
\{v_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\in\mathcal{I}\}
of
V
satisfies the condition

\alpha\leq\beta\;\;\Longrightarrow\;\;|v_{\alpha}|\leq|v_{\beta}|\,.

A cdga
A
is
semifree
or
minimal
if the unit map
\eta\colon\mathbb{Q}\to A
is so.

There is a well-known model structure on the category
\mathrm{cDGA}
that enables us to do homotopy theory with cdgas
[
BG76
, Section 4]
.
A map of cdgas
f\colon A\to B
is a
weak equivalence
or
fibration
if the underlying map of cochain complexes is a quasi-isomorphism or epimorphism respectively.
Cofibrations in
\mathrm{cDGA}
are the morphisms that have the left lifting property with respect to acyclic fibrations.

Remark 3.3
.

The category
Ch^{\geq 0}
of non-negatively graded rational cochain complexes admits a cofibrantly generated model structure for which weak equivalences are quasi-isomorphisms,
fibrations are degreewise epimorphisms, and
cofibrations are monomorphisms in all positive degrees.
Generating sets of cofibrations and acyclic cofibrations for
Ch^{\geq 0}
are given in terms of the
sphere
and
disk
complexes
S(n)
and
D(n)
.
The sphere complex
S(n)
is generated by a single closed element of degree
n
.
The disk complex
D(n)
is generated by elements
a
and
b
in degrees
n-1
and
n
respectively, with
da=b
.
A set of generating cofibrations for
Ch^{\geq 0}
is

\mathcal{I}=\{i_{n}\colon S(n)\to D(n)\mid n\geq 1\}\cup\{i_{0}\colon 0\to S(0)\}\,

where
i_{n}
is the evident inclusion; a set of generating acyclic cofibrations is

\mathcal{J}=\{j_{n}\colon 0\to D(n)\mid n\geq 1\}\,.

The aforementioned model structure on
\mathrm{cDGA}
is equivalent to that produced by transferring the model structure of
Ch^{\geq 0}
along the free-forgetful adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ch^{\geq 0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{cDGA}\,.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

In particular, the model structure on
\mathrm{cDGA}
is cofibrantly generated with generating set of cofibrations
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{cDGA}}=\{\Lambda(i_{n})\mid n\geq 0\}
and of acyclic cofibrations
\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{cDGA}}=\{\Lambda(j_{n})\mid n\geq 1\}
.

Remark 3.4
.

By the previous remark, any cofibration in
\mathrm{cDGA}
is a retract of a relative
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{cDGA}}
-cell complex and conversely.
In particular, minimal and semifree morphisms of cdgas are cofibrations.
The utility of minimal morphisms stems from the fact that any morphism of cdgas
f\colon A\to B
can be factored as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes\Lambda V}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}

where
i
is minimal and
f^{\prime}
is a quasi-isomorphism.
The quasi-isomorphism
f^{\prime}
is a
minimal model
of
f
and minimal models are unique up to isomorphism—both existence and uniqueness of minimal models are proven by a version of the small object argument, working degree-by-degree.
Finally, a
minimal model
for a cdga
A
is a minimal model for the unit
\eta\colon\mathbb{Q}\to A
.

Sullivan’s approach to rational homotopy theory begins by assigning to each simplicial set
X
a cdga computing the rational cohomology algebra of
X
.
To the combinatorial
n
-simplex
\Delta^{n}
we assign the cdga of
polynomial differential forms

\mathfrak{A}_{n}:=\Lambda(t_{0},\dotsc,t_{n},y_{0},\dotsc,y_{n})\big/J_{n}\,,

where the generators satisfy
|t_{i}|=0
and
dt_{i}=y_{i}
for all
i
, and we quotient by the differential ideal
J_{n}
generated by
\langle 1-\sum_{i=0}^{n}t_{i}
and
\sum_{i=0}^{n}y_{i}
.
This construction gives rise to a simplicial cdga

\mathfrak{A}_{\bullet}\colon\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}\longmapsto\mathrm{cDGA}

with face and degeneracy maps given by

\partial_{i}\colon\mathfrak{A}_{n}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{A}_{n-1}\,,\qquad\partial_{i}\colon t_{k}\longmapsto\begin{cases}t_{k}&k<i\\
0&k=i\\
t_{k-1}&k>i\end{cases}

(3.1)

and

s_{i}\colon\mathfrak{A}_{n}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{A}_{n+1}\,,\qquad s_{i}\colon t_{k}\longmapsto\begin{cases}t_{k}&k<i\\
t_{k}+t_{k+1}&k=i\\
t_{k+1}&k>i\,.\end{cases}

(3.2)

Viewing
\mathfrak{A}_{\bullet}
as a cosimplicial object of
\mathrm{cDGA}^{\mathrm{op}}
, the cdga of
piecewise linear (PL) de Rham forms
on a simplicial set
X
is obtained as the coend (in
\mathrm{cDGA}^{\mathrm{op}}
)

\mathfrak{A}(X):=\int^{[n]\in\Delta}X_{n}\times\mathfrak{A}_{n}\cong\mathrm{sSet}\big(X,\mathfrak{A}_{\bullet}\big)\,.

This assignment determines a functor
\mathfrak{A}\colon\mathrm{sSet}\to\mathrm{cDGA}^{\mathrm{op}}
which, according to the yoga of nerve and realisation, participates in the
Sullivan–de Rham adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sSet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{cDGA}^{\mathrm{op}}\,.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{S}}

(3.3)

The right adjoint
\mathfrak{S}
sends a cdga
A
to its
spatial realisation
, which is the simplicial set

\mathfrak{S}(A)\colon[n]\longmapsto\mathrm{cDGA}(A,\mathfrak{A}_{n})\,.

Remark 3.5
.

For any simplicial set
X
there is a natural
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism
\mathfrak{A}(X)\rightsquigarrow C^{\bullet}(X)
between the PL de Rham forms on
X
and the rational cochain complex
[
BG76
, Proposition 3.3]
.
The underlying quasi-isomorphism is the
Stokes map

\rho_{X}\colon\mathfrak{A}(X)\to C^{\bullet}(X)
determined by

\langle\rho_{X}\omega,\sigma\rangle=\int_{\Delta^{n}}\omega(\sigma)

for an
n
-simplex
\sigma\colon\Delta^{n}\to X
.
The Stokes map induces an isomorphism of cohomology algebras
H^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{A}(X))\to H^{\bullet}(X)
.

Example 3.6
.

We consider minimal models for two important families of spaces:

•

Spheres.
For
n=2k+1
odd, a minimal model of the cdga
\mathfrak{A}(S^{n})
is given by the free algebra
\Lambda\langle\omega_{n}\rangle
spanned by a single closed generator
\omega_{n}
of degree
n
.
For
n=2k
even, a minimal model of
\mathfrak{A}(S^{n})
is given by the semifree algebra
\Lambda\langle\omega_{n},\omega_{2n-1}\rangle
spanned by a closed generator
\omega_{n}
of degree
n
and a generator
\omega_{2n-1}
of degree
2n-1
such that
d\omega_{2n-1}=\omega_{n}\wedge\omega_{n}
.

•

Eilenberg–Mac Lane spaces.
For all
n\geq 1
, a minimal model of the cdga
\mathfrak{A}(K(\mathbb{Q},n))
is given by the algebra
\Lambda\langle\omega_{n}\rangle
spanned by a single closed generator in degree
n
.

For odd
n
, the minimal models for
\mathfrak{A}(S^{n})
and
\mathfrak{A}(K(\mathbb{Q},n))
coincide.

Associated to any pair of cdgas
A,B
there is a simplicial
function space

F(A,B):=\mathrm{cDGA}(A,\mathfrak{A}_{\bullet}\otimes B)

of maps
A\to B
.
If
i\colon A\to B
is a cofibration of cdgas and
p\colon X\to Y
is a fibration, the evident map

F(B,X)\longrightarrow F(A,X)\prod_{F(A,Y)}F(B,Y)

is a fibration of simplicial sets which is moreover a weak equivalence if either of
i
or
p
is
[
BG76
, Proposition 5.3]
.
Since
\mathfrak{S}(A)=F(A,\mathbb{Q})
, it follows that the Sullivan–de Rham adjunction (
3.3
) is a Quillen adjunction.

The Sullivan–de Rham adjunction restricts to an adjoint equivalence between simplicial and algebraic versions of the rational homotopy category.
A connected, nilpotent space
X
is
of finite rational type
if the following equivalent conditions are satisfied:

•

The rational vector spaces
H_{1}(X)
and
\pi_{n}X\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
for
n\geq 2
are finite dimensional.

•

The rational vector spaces
H_{n}(X)
for
n\geq 1
are finite dimensional.

A connected space
X
is nilpotent and of finite rational type if and only if it is rationally homotopy equivalent (or, equivalently, rationally homology equivalent) to a space
X^{\prime}
for which the terminal map from each Postnikov section
X^{\prime}_{\leq n}\to\ast
factors as the composite of finitely many principal fibrations with fibres of the form
K(\mathbb{Q},r)
where
r\geq 1
.
A space
X^{\prime}
of this latter form is called a
rational
nilpotent space of finite type, and we write
Ho(\mathcal{S})^{\mathbb{Q}}_{\mathrm{nil,f.t.}}\hookrightarrow Ho(\mathcal{S})
for the full subcategory spanned by such spaces.

On the algebraic side, we say that a cofibrant cdga
A
is
(homologically) connected
if the unit
\mathbb{Q}\to A
induces an isomorphism in
\mathbb{Q}\to H^{0}(A)
.
Such a cdga is moreover of
finite homotopical type
if the minimal model
N\to A
is finite dimensional in each dimension.
Equivalently, a cofibrant connected cdga
A
is of finite homotopical type precisely if each of the vector spaces

\pi^{n}(A):=H^{n}(QA)

is finite dimensional.
Here,
Q
is the complex of indecomposables which is given in terms of the augmentation ideal
\overline{A}=\mathrm{aug}_{\mathbb{Q}}(A)=\mathrm{coker}(\mathbb{Q}\to A)
as
QA=\overline{A}/(\overline{A}\cdot\overline{A})
.
The full subcategory of
Ho(\mathrm{cDGA})
spanned by the (cofibrant) connected cdgas of finite homotopical type is written
Ho(\mathrm{cDGA})_{\mathrm{f.t.,}\geq 1}\hookrightarrow Ho(\mathrm{cDGA})
.

Remark 3.7
.

The functor
A\mapsto\pi^{\ast}(A)
on augmented algebras sends weak equivalences between cofibrant cdgas to isomorphisms of graded rational vector spaces.
If
A
is a cofibrant connected cdga of finite homotopical type, for each
n\geq 1
there is an isomorphism of rational vector spaces

\pi_{n}\mathfrak{S}(A)\cong\mathrm{Hom}(\pi^{n}A,\mathbb{Q})\,,

which is moreover a group isomorphism for
n\geq 2

[
BG76
, Proposition 8.13]
.

Using the previous remark and the minimal models of Example
3.6
, one proves that the derived unit and counit of the Sullivan–de Rham adjunction
(\mathfrak{A}\dashv\mathfrak{S})
are, respectively, rational homotopy equivalences and quasi-isomorphism for the Eilenberg–Mac Lane spaces
K(\mathbb{Q},n)
and their associated minimal models.
Working stage-by-stage in the Postnikov tower by means of an Eilenberg–Moore theorem for polynomial differential forms, Bousfield and Gugenheim prove the following

Theorem 3.8
.

The derived Sullivan–de Rham adjunction restricts to an equivalence of categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ho(\mathcal{S})^{\mathbb{Q}}_{\mathrm{nil,f.t.}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ho(\mathrm{cDGA})^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mathrm{f.t.,}\geq 1}\,.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{L}\mathfrak{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{R}\mathfrak{S}}

We shall also need the following property of the Sullivan–de Rham functor
\mathfrak{A}
.

Lemma 3.9
.

For any simplicial sets
K,L
there is a natural quasi-isomorphism of commutative differential graded algebras
\mathfrak{A}(K)\otimes\mathfrak{A}(L)\to\mathfrak{A}(K\times L)
.

Proof.

The projections
K\times L\to K
and
K\times L\to L
induce maps of cdgas
\mathfrak{A}(K)\to\mathfrak{A}(K\times L)
and
\mathfrak{A}(L)\to\mathfrak{A}(K\times L)
which, by Remark
3.5
, realise the projections in rational cohomology.
The Künneth theorem implies that
\mathfrak{A}(K)\otimes\mathfrak{A}(L)\to\mathfrak{A}(K\times L)
is a quasi-isomorphism.
Naturality of this quasi-isomorphism is evident from the functoriality both of
\mathfrak{A}
and of the projection maps.
∎

3.1
Relative Sullivan–de Rham adjunctions

In this section we examine relative versions of the Sullivan–de Rham adjunction obtained by passing to retractive objects.
Having already encountered the topological protagonists of these adjunctions—the categories of retractive spaces
\mathrm{sSet}_{/\!\!/X}
discussed above—we now briefly introduce the participants on the algebraic side of the story.
For any cdga
A
there is a model category of
augmented
A
-algebras

\mathrm{cDGA}_{/\!\!/A}:=(\mathrm{cDGA}^{A/})_{/\mathrm{id}_{A}}

whose objects are commuting diagrams of cdgas

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}_{A}}

with the evident notion of morphism.
The model structure of
\mathrm{cDGA}_{/\!\!/A}
is created by the forgetful functor to
\mathrm{cDGA}
in the sense that a map of augmented
A
-algebras
B\to B^{\prime}
is a weak equivalence, fibration, or cofibration precisely if the underlying map of cdgas is so.

Proposition 3.10
.

For each cdga
A
there is a Quillen adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sSet}_{/\!\!/\mathfrak{S}(A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathrm{cDGA}_{/\!\!/A})^{\mathrm{op}}\,.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}_{/\!\!/A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{S}_{/\!\!/A}}

(3.4)

Proof sketch.

This a routine application of the fact that Quillen adjunctions induce Quillen adjunctions on slice and coslice categories.

In slightly more detail, the left adjoint
\mathfrak{A}_{/\!\!/A}
sends a retractive space
\mathfrak{S}(A)\xrightarrow{i}Z\xrightarrow{p}\mathfrak{S}(A)
to the augmented
A
-algebra given by the top horizontal row in the following iterated pullback diagram of cdgas:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}_{/\!\!/A}(Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}(Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(A)\,.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}(p)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}(i)}

The right adjoint
\mathfrak{S}_{/\!\!/A}
sends an augmented
A
-algebra
A\to B\to A
to
\mathfrak{S}(A)\leftarrow\mathfrak{S}(B)\leftarrow\mathfrak{S}(A)
.
The unit and counit natural transformations of the adjunction
(\mathfrak{A}_{/\!\!/A}\dashv\mathfrak{S}_{/\!\!/A})
arise from the unit and counit transformations of
(\mathfrak{A}\dashv\mathfrak{S})
in the obvious way.

That the adjunction
(\mathfrak{A}_{/\!\!/A}\dashv\mathfrak{S}_{/\penalty A})
is Quillen is a consequence of the fact that
\mathfrak{S}_{/\!\!/A}
is given on underlying objects by
\mathfrak{S}
, so preserves fibrations and acyclic fibrations.
∎

Given a morphism of cdgas
f\colon A\to B
, there is a functor
f^{\ast}\colon\mathrm{cDGA}_{/\!\!/B}\to\mathrm{cDGA}_{/\!\!/A}
given by forming pullbacks along
f
.
Concretely, an augmented
B
-algebra
B\to C\to B
is sent to the augmented
A
-algebra appearing as the top horizontal row of the iterated pullback diagram of cdgas:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\,.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

The functor
f^{\ast}
has left adjoint
f_{!}
acting on objects by
(A\to D\to A)\mapsto(B\to B\bigotimes_{A}D\to B)
.
The adjunction
(f_{!}\dashv f^{\ast})\colon\mathrm{cDGA}_{/\!\!/A}\to\mathrm{cDGA}_{/\!\!/B}
is Quillen; the right adjoint
f^{\ast}
clearly preserves fibrations and acyclic fibrations.

The base change adjunctions between model categories of retractive spaces and augmented relative algebras are compatible in the following sense:

Proposition 3.11
.

For any morphism of cdgas
f\colon A\to B
the diagram of left Quillen functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sSet}_{/\!\!/\mathfrak{S}(B)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{cDGA}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/\!\!/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sSet}_{/\!\!/\mathfrak{S}(A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{cDGA}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/\!\!/A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}_{/\!\!/B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{S}(f)_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}_{/\!\!/A}}

commutes up to natural isomorphism.

Proof.

The functor
\mathfrak{A}\colon\mathrm{sSet}\to\mathrm{cDGA}^{\mathrm{op}}
is a left adjoint, so sends pushouts of simplicial sets to pullbacks of cdgas.
For a retractive space
Z
over
\mathfrak{S}(B)
, applying
\mathfrak{A}
to the pushout diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{S}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{S}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{S}(f)_{!}Z\ }
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{S}(f)}

gives an isomorphism of cdgas
\mathfrak{A}(\mathfrak{S}(f)_{!}Z)\cong\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(A)\prod_{\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(B)}\mathfrak{A}(Z)
from which it follows that
(\mathfrak{A}_{/\!\!/A}\circ\mathfrak{S}(f)_{!})(Z)
is the pullback of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}(Z)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(f)}

Naturality of the
(\mathfrak{A}\dashv\mathfrak{S})
-counit implies that this is the same as the pullback of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}(Z)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(f)}

computing
(f^{\ast}\circ\mathfrak{A}_{/\!\!/B})(Z)
.
We thus obtain an isomorphism
(\mathfrak{A}_{/\!\!/A}\circ\mathfrak{S}(f)_{!})(Z)\cong(f^{\ast}\circ\mathfrak{A}_{/\!\!/B})(Z)
which is manifestly natural in
Z
.
∎

3.2
Homotopy theory of differential graded modules

In this section we discuss model categories of differential graded modules over differential graded algebras, the algebraic arena of our work in Sections
4
and
5
below.

Definition 3.12
.

Let
A
be a cdga over
\mathbb{Q}
.
A
differential graded
A
-module
over
A
(or simply
A
-module
) is a rational cochain complex
(M^{\ast},d_{M})
equipped with a linear map of degree zero
\rho_{M}\colon A\otimes M\to M
,
(a,m)\mapsto a\cdot m
, subject to the conditions that

•

a\cdot(b\cdot m)=(a\cdot b)\cdot m
for all
a,b\in A
and
m\in M
,

•

1\cdot m=m
for all
m\in M
, with
1\in A
the unit, and

•

d_{M}(a\cdot m)=d_{A}a\cdot m+(-1)^{|a|}a\cdot d_{M}m
for all
a\in A
and
m\in M
.

The data
(M^{\ast},d_{M},\rho_{M})
are frequently abbreviated to
M
, with differential
d_{M}
and
A
-module structure
\rho_{M}
understood from context.
We do not assume
M^{\ast}
to be connective, so that the grading is over
\mathbb{Z}
.

A morphism
f\colon M\to N
of
A
-modules is a map of cochain complexes
f\colon(M^{\ast},d_{M})\to(N^{\ast},d_{N})
such that
f\circ\rho_{M}=\rho_{N}\circ(\mathrm{id}_{A}\otimes f)
.
The
A
-modules are organised into a category, denoted by
A\mathrm{-Mod}
.

For any cdga
A
,
A\mathrm{-Mod}
is equipped with a natural projective model structure.
In this projective model structure, a morphism of
A
-modules
f\colon M\to N
is a
weak equivalence
or
fibration
if the underlying map of cochain complexes is a quasi-isomorphism or degreewise epimorphism respectively.
Cofibrations in
A\mathrm{-Mod}
are morphisms that have the left lifting property with respect to acyclic fibrations.

Remark 3.13
.

The category
Ch
of unbounded rational cochain complexes admits a cofibrantly generated model structure for which the weak equivalences are quasi-isomorphisms and fibrations are degreewise epimorphisms.
A set of generating cofibrations is given in terms of the sphere and disk complexes (cf. Remark
3.3
) by
\mathcal{I}=\{i_{n}\colon S(n)\to D(n)\mid n\in\mathbb{Z}\}
,
with a set of generating acyclic cofibrations given by
\mathcal{J}=\{j_{n}\colon 0\to D(n)\mid n\in\mathbb{Z}\}
.

For any cdga
A
the free-forgetful adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ch}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\mathrm{-Mod}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

induces a transferred model structure on
A\mathrm{-Mod}
.
This transferred model structure coincides with the projective model structure so that
A\mathrm{-Mod}
is cofibrantly generated with generating cofibrations
\mathcal{I}_{A}=\{A\otimes i_{n}\mid n\in\mathbb{Z}\}
and generating acyclic cofibrations
\mathcal{J}_{A}=\{A\otimes j_{n}\mid n\in\mathbb{Z}\}
.

Definition 3.14
.

A morphism of
A
-modules
f\colon M\to N
is
semifree
if the following conditions are satisfied:

(i)

The underlying graded
A^{\ast}
-module of
N^{\ast}
is
M^{\ast}\oplus(A^{\ast}\otimes V)
for some graded rational vector space
V
.

(ii)

V
has a basis
\{v_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\in\mathcal{I}\}
for some well-ordered set
\mathcal{I}
and the differential
d_{N}
on
N
is such that for all
\beta\in\mathcal{I}
,
d_{B}v_{\beta}
lies in the
A
-submodule
M\oplus(A\otimes V_{<\beta})
, with
V_{<\beta}=\mathrm{span}\{v_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\beta\}
.

(iii)

The morphism of
A
-modules
f\colon M\to N
is given on underlying cochain complexes by the evident inclusion
M\to M\oplus(A\otimes V)
.

A semifree morphism of
A
-modules
f\colon M\to N
is moreover
minimal
if the basis
\{v_{\alpha}\mid\alpha\in\mathcal{I}\}
of
V
satisfies the additional condition

\alpha\leq\beta\,\Longrightarrow\;|v_{\alpha}|\leq|v_{\beta}|\,.

An
A
-module
M
is
semifree
or
minimal
if the trivial map of
A
-modules
0\to M
is so.

Remark 3.15
.

Any semifree morphism of
A
-modules is a cofibration.
Indeed, for a semifree map
f\colon M\to N
, parts (ii) and (iii) of Definition

3.14
are equivalent to expressing
f
as the colimit of a sequence of maps

M\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow N_{\alpha}\longrightarrow N_{\alpha+1}\longrightarrow\dotsb

where
N_{\alpha}=M\oplus(A\otimes V_{<(\alpha+1)})
and there is a pushout diagram of
A
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes S(|v_{\alpha+1}|+1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes D(|v_{\alpha+1}|+1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{\alpha+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces dv_{\alpha+1}}

(3.5)

for each
\alpha\in\mathcal{I}
.
The condition that
f
is minimal is equivalent to requiring that the degrees of the disk attachments (
3.5
) is a monotonically increasing function of
\alpha
.

Example 3.16
.

From the characterisation of minimality given above it follows that the minimal
\mathbb{Q}
-modules are precisely the bounded below cochain complexes with trivial differential.

We shall make use of some important properties of minimal maps of
A
-modules.
In the case of connective
A
-modules (cf. Definiton
3.19
below), proofs of these properties can be found in the appendix to
[
RS00
]
.
Essentially the same proofs apply in the present setting.

Lemma 3.17
.

Let
A
be a cdga and let
f\colon M\to N
be a morphism of
A
-modules.
If there is some integer
k
such that
H^{p}(f)\colon H^{p}(M)\to H^{p}(N)
is an isomorphism for
p<k
and a monomorphism for
p=k
then
f
factorises as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M\oplus(A\otimes V)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}

where
i
is minimal and
f^{\prime}
is a quasi-isomorphism.
In particular, any bounded below
A
-module has a minimal model.

Lemma 3.18
.

Let
A
be a cdga and
f\colon M\to N
a map of
A
-modules.
If a minimal model for
f
exists, it is unique up to isomorphism fixing
M
.

Definition 3.19
.

An
A
-module
M
is
connective
if the underlying cochain complex
M^{\ast}
is connective, that is
M^{k}=0
for
k<0
.
The full subcategory of
A\mathrm{-Mod}
spanned by the connective
A
-modules is written
A\mathrm{-Mod}_{\geq 0}
.

Remark 3.20
.

The inclusion
A\mathrm{-Mod}_{\geq 0}\hookrightarrow A\mathrm{-Mod}
preserves all small limits and hence has a left adjoint
\mathrm{cn}^{0}_{A}
by the adjoint functor theorem.
The behaviour of
\mathrm{cn}^{0}_{A}
on general
A
-modules is not straightforward though an explicit description can be given using monadicity.
For free
A
-modules
M=A\otimes V
we have that
\mathrm{cn}^{0}_{A}(M)\cong A\otimes\mathrm{cn}^{0}V
is the free
A
-module on the cochain complex

\mathrm{cn}^{0}V=\Big[\hbox to226.97pt{\vbox to18.96pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 113.48427pt\lower-8.97974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

with
d^{\prime}
the unique map induced by the differential on the quotient.

Example 3.21
.

For a minimal
A
-module
A\otimes V
, let
\{v_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\mathcal{I}}
be a basis for
V
indexed by a well-ordered set
\mathcal{I}
subject to the conditions that (i)
dv_{\alpha}\in A\otimes V_{<\alpha}
and (ii)
\alpha\leq\beta
implies
|v_{\alpha}|\leq|v_{\beta}|
for all
\alpha,\beta\in\mathcal{I}
.
The connective cover of
A\otimes V
is determined by the cofibre sequence of
A
-modules

A\otimes V^{\leq-1}\longrightarrow A\otimes V\longrightarrow\mathrm{cn}^{0}_{A}(A\otimes V)\,,

where
A\otimes V^{\leq-1}
is the minimal
A
-submodule generated by the
v_{\alpha}
for which
|v_{\alpha}|\leq-1
.

For a map of cdgas
f\colon B\to A
, the
augmentation ideal
of
B
is the kernel
\mathrm{aug}_{A}(B):=\ker(f)
.
In the case that
B\in\mathrm{cDGA}_{/\!\!/A}
is an augmented
A
-algebra, the underlying cochain complex of
B
splits as
B\cong A\oplus\mathrm{aug}_{A}(B)
and the augmentation ideal inherits the structure of an
A
-module.
It is easy to check that the resulting functor

\mathrm{aug}_{A}\colon\mathrm{cDGA}_{/\!\!/A}\longrightarrow A\mathrm{-Mod}_{\geq 0}

preserves limits and so has a left adjoint
\Lambda_{A}
by the adjoint functor theorem.
The left adjoint
\Lambda_{A}
computes free
A
-algebras; we do not need an explicit description in the general case, but note that for a free
A
-module
M\cong A\otimes V
we have
\Lambda_{A}(M)\cong A\otimes\Lambda V
.

In summary, for each cdga
A
we have a composite adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\mathrm{-Mod}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\mathrm{-Mod}_{\geq 0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{cDGA}_{/\!\!/A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{cn}^{0}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{aug}_{A}}

(3.6)

By an abuse of notation we write this adjunction as
(\Lambda_{A}\dashv\mathrm{aug}_{A})
.

Proposition 3.22
.

The adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\mathrm{-Mod}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{cDGA}_{/\!\!/A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{aug}_{A}}

is Quillen.

Proof.

Let
f\colon B\to C
be a map of augmented
A
-algebras.
The underlying map of chain complexes splits as
\mathrm{id}\oplus\mathrm{aug}_{A}f\colon A\oplus\mathrm{aug}_{A}B\to A\oplus\mathrm{aug}_{A}C
and it is clear that
f
is a quasi-isomorphism or epimorphism precisely if
\mathrm{aug}_{A}f
is.
Thus
\mathrm{aug}_{A}
preserves fibrations and acyclic fibrations and so the adjunction is Quillen.
∎

Associated to a morphism of cdgas
f\colon A\to B
there is a base change adjunction relating the module categories
(f_{!}\dashv f^{\ast})\colon A\mathrm{-Mod}\to B\mathrm{-Mod}
.
The left adjoint sends an
A
-module
M
to the
B
-module
f_{!}(M):=B\otimes_{A}M
and the right adjoint regards a
B
-module
N
as an
A
-module via restriction along
f
.
Since
f^{\ast}
leaves the underlying cochain complexes fixed it is evidently a right Quillen functor.
Note that we use the same symbols to denote base change functors between categories of modules and augmented relative algebras.
This mild abuse of notation is justified by the following

Proposition 3.23
.

For any morphism of cdgas
f\colon A\to B
the diagram of left Quillen functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\mathrm{-Mod}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{cDGA}_{/\!\!/A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathrm{-Mod}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{cDGA}_{/\!\!/B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{B}}

commutes up to natural isomorphism.

Proof.

By essential uniqueness of adjoints, to prove the claim it is sufficient to check that there is a natural isomorphism of composite right adjoints
\mathrm{aug}_{A}\circ f^{\ast}\cong f^{\ast}\circ\mathrm{aug}_{B}
.
For any augmented
B
-algebra
C
we have a natural splitting of cochain complexes
C\cong B\oplus\mathrm{aug}_{B}C
which implies that the underlying cochain complex of the augmented
A
-algebra
f^{\ast}C
naturally splits as
f^{\ast}C\cong A\oplus\mathrm{aug}_{B}C
.
By construction, the induced
A
-module structure on
\mathrm{aug}_{A}(f^{\ast}C)\cong\mathrm{aug}_{B}C
coincides with the restriction of the
B
-module structure along
f
.
∎

Remark 3.24
.

For a morphism of cdgas
f\colon A\to B
, extension of scalars
f_{!}\colon A\mathrm{-Mod}\to B\mathrm{-Mod}
preserves semifreeness and minimality.
This is an easy consequence of the characterisations of these properties given in Remark
3.15
.
In the case that
f\colon A\to\mathbb{Q}
is an augmentation, any minimal
A
-module
A\otimes V
is thus sent by
f_{!}
to
V
regarded as a chain complex with trivial differential.

3.3
Modular models and fibrewise suspensions

3.4
) and (
3.6

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{45.54166pt}{-11.15274pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

(3.7)

We will later construct algebraic models for rational parametrised spectra by stabilising these adjunctions over a fixed cdga
A
(the superscript “
u
” is for “unstable”).
In order to do this, we must first examine modules in the image of
\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}
and how this functor behaves under forming fibrewise suspensions.

For a retractive space
Z
over
\mathfrak{S}(A)
, the underlying cochain complex of
\mathfrak{A}(Z)
splits naturally as
\mathfrak{A}(Z)\cong\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(A)\oplus\mathrm{aug}_{\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(A)}\mathfrak{A}(Z)
.
Since
\mathfrak{A}_{/\!\!/Z}
is constructed by pulling back
\mathfrak{A}(Z)\to\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(A)
along the Sullivan–de Rham counit
A\to\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(A)
, it follows that

\mathrm{aug}_{A}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{/\!\!/A}(Z)\big)\cong\mathrm{aug}_{\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(A)}\big(\mathfrak{A}(Z)\big)

as
A
-modules.
Further, the Sullivan–de Rham functor
\mathfrak{A}\colon\mathrm{sSet}\to\mathrm{cDGA}^{\mathrm{op}}
sends the pushout diagram of simplicial sets

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{S}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ast}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z/\mathfrak{S}(A)}

to a pullback diagram of cdgas.
Since
Z/\mathfrak{S}(A)
is pointed, the underlying cochain complex of
\mathfrak{A}(Z/\mathfrak{S}(A))
splits as
\mathfrak{A}(Z/\mathfrak{S}(A))\cong\mathbb{Q}\oplus\mathrm{aug}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{A}(Z/\mathfrak{S}(A)))
and there is a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{aug}_{\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(A)}(\mathfrak{A}(Z))\cong\mathrm{aug}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{A}(Z/\mathfrak{S}(A))
of
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(A)
-modules.
Combined with the above, we get a natural isomorphism of
A
-modules

\mathrm{aug}_{A}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{/\!\!/A}(Z)\big)\cong\mathrm{aug}_{\mathbb{Q}}\big(\mathfrak{A}(Z/\mathfrak{S}(A)\big)

so that
\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}
sends
Z
to the
A
-module
\mathrm{aug}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{A}(Z/\mathfrak{S}(A))
.
The next result provides a topological interpretation of this
A
-module under certain mild conditions.

Proposition 3.25
.

Let
A
be a cofibrant connected cdga of finite homotopical type.
For any retractive space
Z
over
\mathfrak{S}(A)
, the
H^{\bullet}(A)
-action on
H^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z))
is naturally isomorphic to the
H^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{S}(A))
-action on the reduced rational cohomology
\widetilde{H}^{\bullet}(Z/\mathfrak{S}(A))
.

Proof.

For any simplicial set
X
and retractive space
Z\in\mathrm{sSet}_{/\!\!/X}
, applying the rational cochains functor to the iterated pushout diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ast}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z/X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ast}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

(3.8)

gives splittings of cochain complexes

C^{\bullet}(Z)\cong C^{\bullet}(X)\oplus M

and
C^{\bullet}(Z/X)\cong\mathbb{Q}\oplus M^{\prime}
.
With respect to these splittings the quotient map
Z\to Z/X
gives rise to the map
C^{\bullet}(Z/X)\to C^{\bullet}(Z)
given as the sum of the unit
\mathbb{Q}\to C^{\bullet}(X)
and a quasi-isomorphism
M^{\prime}\to M
.

The
H^{\bullet}(X)
-module structure on
\widetilde{H}^{\bullet}(Z/X)
is induced by the space-level coaction

\displaystyle Z/X

\displaystyle\longrightarrow X_{+}\wedge Z/X

\displaystyle[z]

\displaystyle\longmapsto p(z)\wedge[z]\,.

Examining the commuting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{+}\wedge Z_{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{+}\wedge Z_{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z/X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{+}\wedge Z/X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{+}\wedge\mathrm{id}_{Z_{+}}}

we find that the
H^{\bullet}(X)
-module structure on
\widetilde{H}^{\bullet}(Z/X)
is compatible with the
H^{\bullet}(X)
-module structure on
H^{\bullet}(Z)
induced by the coaction
Z\to X\times Z
.
Taking cohomology, the
M^{\prime}\to M
thus becomes an isomorphism of
H^{\bullet}(X)
-modules.

Applying the Sullivan–de Rham functor
\mathfrak{A}
to the diagram (
3.8
) we get splittings of cochain complexes
\mathfrak{A}(Z)\cong\mathfrak{A}(X)\oplus\mathrm{aug}_{\mathfrak{A}(X)}\mathfrak{A}(Z)
and
\mathfrak{A}(Z/X)\cong\mathbb{Q}\oplus\mathrm{aug}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathfrak{A}(Z/X)
.
By (
3.8
), the components of the natural
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism
\mathfrak{A}(Y)\rightsquigarrow C^{\bullet}(Y)
(Remark
3.5
) at
Z
and
Z/X
respect the splittings and hence we obtain a commuting diagram of quasi-isomorphisms of cochain complexes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{aug}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathfrak{A}(Z/X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{aug}_{\mathfrak{A}(X)}\mathfrak{A}(Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}

Since the vertical arrows arise from
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphisms, all arrows in the above diagram are isomorphisms of
H^{\bullet}(X)\cong H^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{A}(X))
-modules in cohomology.

In the case that
X=\mathfrak{S}(A)
is the spatial realisation of a cofibrant connected cdga
A
of finite rational type, the Sullivan–de Rham counit
A\to\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(A)
is a weak equivalence by the Sullivan–de Rham equivalence theorem.
For a retractive space
Z\in\mathrm{sSet}_{/\!\!/\mathfrak{S}(A)}
, the
A
-module structure on
\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)
is obtained by restricting the
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(A)
-module structure on
\mathrm{aug}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathfrak{A}(Z/\mathfrak{S}(A))
considered above along the counit
A\to\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(A)
.
With this observation, the result follows.
∎

Example 3.26
.

Consider the case that
Z=Y_{X+}
for some
p\colon Y\to X
(cf. Example
2.8
).
In this case we have
Z/X\cong Y_{+}
and
\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)\cong\mathfrak{A}(Y)
regarded as an
A
-module by restriction along
A\to\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{S}(A)=\mathfrak{A}(X)\to\mathfrak{A}(Y)
.

We now seek to understand how
\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}
interacts with fibrewise stabilisation.
To ease the notation we adopt the following convention:

For the remainder of this section we write
X:=\mathfrak{S}(A)
for the spatial realisation of
A
.

As
\Sigma_{X}Z
coincides with the cofibre of
\partial\Delta^{1}\otimes_{X}Z\to\Delta^{1}\otimes_{X}Z
(cf. Remark
2.18
), our first step is to analyse how
\mathfrak{A}_{/\!\!/A}
interacts with finite
\mathrm{sSet}
-tensors.

Lemma 3.27
.

For any simplicial set
K
and
Z\in\mathrm{sSet}_{/\!\!/X}
there is a natural quasi-isomorphism of
A
-modules

\mathfrak{A}(K)\otimes\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(K\otimes_{X}Z)

with
A
acting on the second tensor factor of
\mathfrak{A}(K)\otimes\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)
.

Proof.

There is a commuting diagram of simplicial sets in which each square is a pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\times X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ast}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\times Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\otimes_{X}Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(K\times Z)/(K\times X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\times Z/X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\times X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ast}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}

(3.9)

which, upon applying the Sullivan–de Rham functor
\mathfrak{A}
, gives rise to isomorphisms of cochain complexes

\mathrm{aug}_{\mathfrak{A}(K\times X)}\mathfrak{A}(K\times Z)\cong\mathrm{aug}_{\mathfrak{A}(X)}\mathfrak{A}(K\otimes_{X}Z)\cong\mathrm{aug}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathfrak{A}((K\times Z)/(K\times X))\cong\mathrm{aug}_{\mathfrak{A}(K)}\mathfrak{A}(K\times Z/X)

compatible with the various module structures.
By Lemma
3.9
, associated to the right hand column in the diagram (
3.9
) there is a commuting diagram of cdgas

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}(K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}(K\times Z/X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}(K)\otimes\mathfrak{A}(Z/X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}(K)\,,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varpi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

in which
\varpi
is a quasi-isomorphism and the left hand vertical and bottom horizontal arrows arise from
\mathbb{Q}\to\mathfrak{A}(Z/X)\to\mathbb{Q}
by tensoring with
\mathfrak{A}(K)
.
Passing to augmentation ideals we get a quasi-isomorphism of cochain complexes

\mathfrak{A}(K)\otimes\mathrm{aug}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathfrak{A}(Z/X)\to\mathrm{aug}_{\mathfrak{A}(K)}\mathfrak{A}(K\times Z/X)
and hence a quasi-isomorphism

\mathfrak{A}(K)\otimes\mathrm{aug}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathfrak{A}(Z/X)\longrightarrow\mathrm{aug}_{\mathfrak{A}(X)}\mathfrak{A}(K\otimes_{X}Z)\,.

Similarly to the proof of Proposition
3.25
, it is easy to show that this quasi-isomorphism respects the
A
-module structures.
There are natural quasi-isomorphisms
\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)\cong\mathrm{aug}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathfrak{A}(Z/X)
and
\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(K\otimes_{X}Z)\cong\mathrm{aug}_{\mathfrak{A}(X)}\mathfrak{A}(K\otimes_{X}Z)
respecting the
A
-module structures.
This observation completes the proof.
∎

The category
\mathrm{sSet}_{/\!\!/X}
is also tensored over pointed simplicial sets, so we have the following

Corollary 3.28
.

For any pointed simplicial set
K
and
Z\in\mathrm{sSet}_{/\!\!/X}
there is a natural quasi-isomorphism of
A
-modules

\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{\mathbb{Q}}(K)\otimes\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(K\varowedge_{X}Z)

with
A
acting on the second tensor factor of
\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{\mathbb{Q}}(K)\otimes\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)
.

Proof.

For a pointed simplicial set
(K,k)
and retractive space
Z
over
X
, the tensoring
K\varowedge_{X}Z
is the cofibre of the map
\ast\otimes_{X}Z\cong Z\to K\otimes_{X}Z
obtained by tensoring the retractive space
Z
with
k\colon\ast\to K
.
Since
\mathfrak{A}(K)\cong\mathbb{Q}\oplus\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{\mathbb{Q}}(K)
as chain complexes, the result follows by the Lemma, the fact that
\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}
sends cofibre sequences to fibre sequences of
A
-modules, and a routine application of the five lemma.
∎

Remark 3.29
.

For any integer
n
, write
\mathbb{Q}[n]
for the rational cochain complex consisting of a single copy of
\mathbb{Q}
concentrated in degree
-n
.
For a cochain complex
M
we write
M[n]:=M\otimes\mathbb{Q}[n]
, so that
M[n]^{k}=M^{k+n}
and
d_{M[n]}=(-1)^{n}d_{M}
.
If
M
is an
A
-module then so is
M[n]
; writing
m\mapsto m[n]
for the canonical isomorphism
M^{k+n}\to M[n]^{k}
the action of a homogeneous element
a
of
A
is given by
a\cdot m[n]:=(-1)^{n\cdot|a|}(a\cdot m)[n]
.

Lemma 3.30
.

Let
A
be a cdga.
For any retractive space
Z
over
X=\mathfrak{S}(A)
there is a natural quasi-isomorphism of
A
-modules

\tau_{Z}\colon\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)[-1]\to\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(\Sigma_{X}Z)
.

Proof.

By Remark
2.18
, the fibrewise suspension
\Sigma_{X}Z
is computed via the pushout diagram in
\mathrm{sSet}_{/\!\!/X}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial\Delta^{1}\otimes_{X}Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{1}\otimes_{X}Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma_{X}Z\,.}

The tensor
\partial\Delta^{1}\otimes_{X}Z=Z\coprod_{X}Z
is the coproduct of two copies of
Z
in
\mathrm{sSet}_{/\!\!/X}
and
X\in\mathrm{sSet}_{/\!\!/X}
is the zero object, hence upon applying
\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}
and invoking Lemma
3.27
we get a morphism of pullback squares of
A
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(\Sigma_{X}Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P(Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(\Delta^{1}\otimes_{X}Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)\oplus\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}_{1}\otimes\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)\oplus\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

(3.10)

with fibrations and quasi-isomorphisms as indicated.
The composite
\mathfrak{A}_{1}\to N^{\bullet}(\Delta^{1})
of the Stokes map with the projection to normalised cochains has a section

\varsigma\colon N^{\bullet}(\Delta^{1})=\bigg[\hbox to198.03pt{\vbox to19.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 99.01382pt\lower-8.15973pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-99.01382pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}}

given in degrees
0
and
1
by the assignments

(a,b)\mapsto at_{0}+bt_{1}
and

respectively.
The section
\varsigma
is a quasi-isomorphism and gives rise to a morphism of pullback squares of
A
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P(Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}[-1]\otimes\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}_{1}\otimes\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)\oplus\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\bullet}(\Delta^{1})\otimes\mathfrak{M}^{u}(Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)\oplus\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

(3.11)

with fibrations and quasi-isomorphisms as indicated.
All objects of
A\mathrm{-Mod}
are fibrant, hence by Kan’s cube lemma (see
[
Hov99
, Lemma 5.2.6]
) the top left morphisms of the diagrams (
3.10
) and (
3.11
) are quasi-isomorphisms.
The composite
\tau_{Z}\colon\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)[-1]\to\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(\Sigma_{X}Z)
is thus a quasi-isomorphism for any retractive space
Z
over
X
.
The naturality of
\tau_{Z}
in
Z
follows from Lemma
3.27
.
∎

Remark 3.31
.

Suppose that
p\colon Z\to X
is a nilpotent fibration of nilpotent spaces of finite rational type.
Supposing that
A
is a Sullivan model for the rational homotopy type of
X
, then
p
is modelled in
\mathrm{cDGA}
by a minimal morphism
A\to\widetilde{A}(Z):=A\otimes\Lambda V
.
If
Z
is a retractive space over
X
then
p
admits a distinguished section, modelled in
\mathrm{cDGA}
by the morphism
A\otimes\Lambda V\to A
sending
V
to
0
.
In this situation, Félix, Murillo and Tanré have shown
[
FMT10
]
that the fibrewise suspension
\Sigma_{X}Z\to X
is modelled by

A\longrightarrow\widetilde{\mathfrak{A}}(\Sigma_{X}Z):=A\otimes\big(\mathbb{Q}\oplus(\Lambda^{\geq 1}V)[-1]\big)

where the product of any pair of elements in
(\Lambda^{\geq 1}V)[-1]
is zero, and if
v\in\Lambda^{\geq 1}V
has differential
dv=\sum_{i}a_{i}\otimes w_{i}
in
A\otimes\Lambda V
, then
d(v[-1])=\sum_{i}(-1)^{|a_{i}|+1}a_{i}\otimes w_{i}[-1]
in
\widetilde{\mathfrak{A}}(\Sigma_{X}Z)
.

Under the simplifying assumption
X=\mathfrak{S}(A)
, there are quasi-isomorphisms of
A
-modules
\mathrm{aug}_{A}\widetilde{\mathfrak{A}}(Z)=A\otimes\Lambda^{\geq 1}V\to\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z)
and
\mathrm{aug}_{A}\widetilde{\mathfrak{A}}(\Sigma_{X}Z)=A\otimes(\Lambda^{\geq 1}V)[-1]\to\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(\Sigma_{X}Z)
.
Taking cohomology we get an isomorphism
H^{\bullet}(A\otimes(\Lambda^{\geq 1}V)[-1])\cong H^{\bullet+1}(A\otimes\Lambda^{\geq 1}V))
, in accordance with the Lemma.

4
Fibrewise stable rational homotopy

We have so far established adjunctions (
3.7
) relating retractive spaces and modules and examined how these adjunctions behave with respect to fibrewise stabilisations.
In this section, we build upon these results to give an algebraic characterisation of the rational homotopy theory of nilpotent parametrised spectra over a nilpotent base space.

In detail, for each cdga
A
we formulate a Quillen adjunction

(\mathfrak{M}_{A}\dashv\mathfrak{P}_{A})\colon\hbox to153.25pt{\vbox to19.29pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 76.62503pt\lower-9.64363pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

(4.1)

which is pseudonatural in
A
and so covers the Sullivan–de Rham adjunction in a certain sense.
These Quillen adjunctions induce derived adjunctions of homotopy categories and the main result of this article, Theorem
4.20
, states that if
A
is cofibrant, connected, and of finite homotopical type the derived adjunctions restrict to an equivalence of categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ho(\mathrm{Sp}_{\mathfrak{S}(A)})^{\mathbb{Q}}_{\mathrm{f.t.,nil,bbl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ho(A\mathrm{-Mod})^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mathrm{f.h.t.}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Here we have that:

•

Ho(A\mathrm{-Mod})^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mathrm{f.t.,bbl}}
is the full subcategory spanned by
A
-modules of
finite homotopical type
, namely those
A
-modules admitting a bounded below minimal model which is finitely generated in each degree (see Definition
4.18
below).

•

Ho(\mathrm{Sp}_{\mathfrak{S}(A)})^{\mathbb{Q}}_{\mathrm{f.t.,nil,bbl}}
is the full category of the rational homotopy theory of
\mathfrak{S}(A)
-spectra spanned by rational parametrised that are:

(i)

of finite type (e.g. the fibrewise rational stable homotopy groups are finite dimensional vector spaces in each degree);

(ii)

nilpotent (e.g.
\pi_{1}\mathfrak{S}(A)
acts nilpotently on fibrewise stable homotopy as in Section
2.2
); and

(iii)

bounded below (e.g. the fibrewise stable homotopy groups vanish in sufficiently large negative degree).

Under the above conditions, the
A
-module
\mathfrak{M}_{A}(P)
associated to any cofibrant
\mathfrak{S}(A)
-spectrum
P
computes the
H^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{S}(A))
-action on the rational cohomology groups of the pushforward
\mathfrak{S}(A)_{!}P
(Proposition
4.10
).
Our result states that under some mild conditions, this
A
-module
\mathfrak{M}_{A}(P)
completely determines
P
up to fibrewise rational homotopy equivalence.

Throughout this section we work with respect to a fixed cdga
A
and we
X:=\mathfrak{S}(A)
for the spatial realisation.

4.1
From spectra to modules

In this section we construct the stabilised adjunction
(\mathfrak{M}_{A}\dashv\mathfrak{P}_{A})
relating sequential
X
-spectra with unbounded
A
-modules and we give a topological interpretation the
A
-module
\mathfrak{M}_{A}
.
For this we require the following auxiliary notion:

Definition 4.1
.

An
A
-module cospectrum
is a sequence of connective
A
-modules
N_{0},N_{1},N_{2},\dotsc
equipped with morphisms of
A
-modules
\varsigma_{n}\colon N_{n+1}\to N_{n}[-1]
for each
n\geq 0
.
A morphism of
A
-module cospectra
f\colon N\to M
is a sequence of
A
-module morphisms
f_{n}\colon N_{n}\to M_{n}
commuting with the structure maps
\varsigma_{n}
.
The category of
A
-module cospectra is written
\mathrm{CoSp}(A\mathrm{-Mod}_{\geq 0})
.

Remark 4.2
.

To ease the notation, we write
s
for the shift functor on
A
-modules
M\mapsto M[-1]
.
The shift functor
s
is an auto-equivalence of
A\mathrm{-Mod}
with inverse equivalence
\ell\colon M\mapsto M[1]
.
In the usual fashion,
s
and
\ell
determine adjoint equivalences of categories
(s\dashv\ell)
and
(\ell\dashv s)
.

While the restriction of
s
to
A\mathrm{-Mod}_{\geq 0}
is no longer an auto-equivalence, it has a left adjoint given in terms of the reflection adjunction
(\mathrm{cn}^{0}_{A}\dashv i)\colon A\mathrm{-Mod}\to A\mathrm{-Mod}_{\geq 0}
by
\ell^{\prime}:=\mathrm{cn}^{0}_{A}\circ\ell\circ i
.
It is clear that if
M
is
1
-connective, i.e.
M^{k}=0
for
k\leq 0
, then
\ell^{\prime}M=\ell M
.

By Lemma
3.30
there is a natural weak equivalence of functors
\mathrm{sSet}_{/\!\!/X}\to A\mathrm{-Mod}^{\mathrm{op}}_{\geq 0}

\tau\colon\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}\circ\Sigma_{X}\xrightarrow{\;\;\cong\;\;}s\circ\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}\,.

Consider the “dual” natural transformation
D\tau\colon\ell\circ\mathfrak{P}^{u}_{A}\to\Omega_{X}\circ\mathfrak{P}^{u}_{A}
with components determined by the adjointness diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\mathrm{-Mod}^{\mathrm{op}}_{\geq 0}\big(s\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(Z),M\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\mathrm{-Mod}^{\mathrm{op}}_{\geq 0}\big(\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(\Sigma_{X}Z),M\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sSet}_{/\!\!/X}\big(Z,\mathfrak{P}^{u}_{A}(\ell^{\prime}M)\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sSet}_{/\!\!/X}\big(Z,\Omega_{X}\mathfrak{P}^{u}_{A}(M)\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D\tau_{M}}

Using
D\tau
we construct a functor
\mathfrak{P}^{\mathbb{N}}_{A}\colon\mathrm{CoSp}(A\mathrm{-Mod}_{\geq 0})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Sp}^{\mathbb{N}}_{X}
as follows.
For an
A
-module cospectrum
N
, the structure maps
N_{n+1}\to sN_{n}
are adjoint to maps
\ell^{\prime}N_{n+1}\to N_{n}
.
We set
\mathfrak{P}^{\mathbb{N}}_{A}(N)_{n}:=\mathfrak{P}^{u}_{A}(N_{n})
, which determines a sequential
X
-spectrum with structure maps adjunct to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{P}^{u}_{A}(N_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{P}^{u}_{A}(\ell^{\prime}N_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{X}\mathfrak{P}^{u}_{A}(N_{n})\,.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D\tau_{N_{n}}}

This assignment on objects determines a functor
\mathfrak{P}^{\mathbb{N}}_{A}\colon\mathrm{CoSp}(A\mathrm{-Mod}_{\geq 0})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Sp}^{\mathbb{N}}_{X}
which has a left adjoint
\mathfrak{M}^{\mathbb{N}}_{A}\colon\mathrm{Sp}_{X}^{\mathbb{N}}\to\mathrm{CoSp}(A\mathrm{-Mod}_{\geq 0})^{\mathrm{op}}
that is somewhat less straightforward to describe.
Given a sequential
X
-spectrum
P
, iterating the natural weak equivalence
\tau
gives rise to weak equivalences of
A
-modules

\tau^{p}\colon s^{p}\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(P_{q})\longrightarrow\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(\Sigma_{X}^{p}P_{q})

for all
p,q\geq 0
.
Define
\mathfrak{M}^{\mathbb{N}}_{A}(P)_{n}
to be the
A
-module given as the equaliser of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\prod_{p+q=n}s^{p}\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(P_{q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\prod_{p+q+r=n}s^{p}\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(\Sigma_{X}^{q}P_{r})}

(4.2)

where the top arrow is induced by applying
s^{p}\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}
to the iterated structure maps
\Sigma_{X}^{p}P_{r}\to P_{p+r}
and the bottom arrow is induced by the maps
s^{p}\tau^{q}\colon s^{p+q}\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(P_{r})\to s^{p}\mathfrak{M}^{u}_{A}(\Sigma_{X}^{q}P_{r})
.
The structure maps
\mathfrak{M}^{\mathbb{N}}_{A}(P)_{n+1}\to s\mathfrak{M}^{\mathbb{N}}_{A}(P)_{n}
are obtained by viewing the equaliser diagram for
s\mathfrak{M}^{\mathbb{N}}_{A}(P)_{n}
as the subdiagram of the equaliser diagram for
\mathfrak{M}^{\mathbb{N}}_{A}(P)_{n+1}
consisting of all terms with at least one
s
.
A tedious but straightforward argument now proves the following

Lemma 4.3
.

For each cdga
A
there is an adjunction

(\mathfrak{M}^{\mathbb{N}}_{A}\dashv\mathfrak{P}^{\mathbb{N}}_{A})\colon\hbox to197.14pt{\vbox to19.29pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 98.57068pt\lower-9.64363pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

such that
\mathfrak{P}^{\mathbb{N}}_{A}(N)_{n}=\mathfrak{P}^{u}_{A}(N_{n})
for any
A
-module cospectrum
